\displaystyle=-\frac{8\pi^{3}|e|^{3}}{\hbar^{2}}\tau_{\text{eff}}^{2}n_{\text{imp}}|\mathbf{E}|^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}^{\prime},\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}}\mathbf{v}_{c\mathbf{q}}(|\hat{e}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{cv,\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}|^{2}\delta(2\hbar vq^{\prime}-\hbar\omega)-[|\hat{e}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{cv,\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}|^{2}\delta(2\hbar vq^{\prime}-\hbar\omega)])

(309)

\displaystyle\times\operatorname{Im}(V_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\mathbf{q}}V_{\mathbf{q}\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}}V_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{q}^{\prime}})\delta(E_{c\mathbf{q}}-E_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}})\delta(E_{c\mathbf{q}}-E_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime}})+c\rightarrow v,

where the
\delta(2\hbar vq^{\prime}-\hbar\omega)
can no longer be taken out of the iso-energy average as in other cases.

Let us focus only on the contribution of the electrons in the conduction band, and the calculations of the contribution of the holes in the valence band are similar. The term containing the optical affinity (instead of its isoenergy average) in the ballistic current can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\frac{|e|^{3}}{\hbar^{2}}\tau_{\text{eff}}^{2}\left(\frac{e^{2}}{\epsilon}\right)^{3}n_{\text{imp}}\frac{1}{k_{scr}^{6}}|\mathbf{E}|^{2}\int d\mathbf{q}d\mathbf{q}^{\prime}d\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}\big(v\sin\theta\cos\phi\hat{x}+v\sin\theta\sin\phi\hat{y}+(v\cos\theta+v_{zt})\hat{z}\big)

(310)

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{4q^{\prime 2}}(1-|\hat{e}\cdot\hat{q}^{\prime}|^{2}+i(\hat{e}\times\hat{\overline{e}})\cdot\hat{q}^{\prime})\operatorname{Im}(\langle u_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}\,|\,u_{c\mathbf{q}}\rangle\langle u_{c\mathbf{q}}\,|\,u_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\langle u_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}}\,|\,u_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}\rangle)\delta(2\hbar vq^{\prime}-\hbar\omega)\delta(E_{c\mathbf{q}}-E_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}})\delta(E_{c\mathbf{q}}-E_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}).

Note that if we define
\alpha=v_{zt}/v
, the Dirac-delta funciton can be expressed as

\delta(E_{c\mathbf{q}}-E_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime}})=\frac{1}{\hbar v}\delta(q+\alpha q\cos\theta-q^{\prime}-\alpha q^{\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\hbar v}\frac{1}{1+\alpha\cos\theta}\delta(q-q^{\prime}\frac{1+\alpha\cos\theta^{\prime}}{1+\alpha\cos\theta}).

(311)

Given that

\operatorname{Im}(\langle u_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}\,|\,u_{\mathbf{q}}\rangle\langle u_{\mathbf{q}}\,|\,u_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\langle u_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}}\,|\,u_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}\rangle)=-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}\times\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{\mathbf{q}}=-\frac{1}{4}\hat{q}^{\prime\prime}\cdot\hat{q}^{\prime}\times\hat{q}

(312)

we can then integrate over
q
,
q^{\prime}
, and
q^{\prime\prime}
to integrate out the Dirac delta functions:

\displaystyle\int d\Omega d\Omega^{\prime}d\Omega^{\prime\prime}v\big(\sin\theta\cos\phi\hat{x}+\sin\theta\sin\phi\hat{y}+(\cos\theta+\alpha)\hat{z}\big)\frac{1}{4q^{\prime 2}}(1-|\hat{e}\cdot\hat{q}|^{2}+i(\hat{e}\times\hat{\overline{e}})\cdot\hat{q})

(313)

\displaystyle\times\left(-\frac{1}{4}\cos\theta^{\prime\prime}\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta(\cos\phi^{\prime}\sin\phi-\sin\phi^{\prime}\cos\phi)\right)

\displaystyle\times\int q^{2}q^{\prime\prime 2}dqdq^{\prime}dq^{\prime\prime}\delta(2\hbar vq^{\prime}-\hbar\omega)\delta(E_{c\mathbf{q}}-E_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}})\delta(E_{c\mathbf{q}}-E_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\int d\Omega d\Omega^{\prime}d\Omega^{\prime\prime}v\big(\sin\theta\cos\phi\hat{x}+\sin\theta\sin\phi\hat{y}+(\cos\theta+\alpha)\hat{z}\big)\frac{1}{4q^{\prime 2}}(1-|\hat{e}\cdot\hat{q}|^{2}+i(\hat{e}\times\hat{\overline{e}})\cdot\hat{q})

\displaystyle\times\left(-\frac{1}{4}\cos\theta^{\prime\prime}\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta(\cos\phi^{\prime}\sin\phi-\sin\phi^{\prime}\cos\phi)\right)

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{2(\hbar v)^{3}}\frac{1}{1+\alpha\cos\theta}\frac{1}{1+\alpha\cos\theta^{\prime\prime}}\left(\frac{\omega}{2v}\frac{1+\alpha\cos\theta^{\prime}}{1+\alpha\cos\theta}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\omega}{2v}\frac{1+\alpha\cos\theta^{\prime}}{1+\alpha\cos\theta^{\prime\prime}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{32}\left(\frac{\omega}{2v}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{\hbar(\hbar v)^{2}}\int d\Omega d\Omega^{\prime}d\Omega^{\prime\prime}\big(\sin\theta\cos\phi\hat{x}+\sin\theta\sin\phi\hat{y}+(\cos\theta+\alpha)\hat{z}\big)(1-|\hat{e}\cdot\hat{q}|^{2}+i(\hat{e}\times\hat{\overline{e}})\cdot\hat{q})

\displaystyle\times\cos\theta^{\prime\prime}\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta(\cos\phi^{\prime}\sin\phi-\sin\phi^{\prime}\cos\phi)\frac{(1+\alpha\cos\theta^{\prime})^{4}}{(1+\alpha\cos\theta)^{3}(1+\alpha\cos\theta^{\prime\prime})^{3}}.

Note that in the first step of the above equation, we only keep
\hat{q}^{\prime\prime}_{z}(\hat{q}^{\prime}\times\hat{q})_{z}
for the imaginary part of the Bargamann invariant, because the Bargamann invariant is the only term in the integrand that depends on
\phi^{\prime\prime}
and
\int d\phi^{\prime\prime}\hat{q}^{\prime\prime}_{x/y}=0

44
44

44

This is also true for n-Weyl for
n>1
, where we should replace
\hat{q}^{\prime\prime}_{x/y}
by
(\hat{d}_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}})_{x/y}
.
. Moreover,
(1-|\hat{e}\cdot\hat{q}^{\prime}|^{2})
can be explicitly evaluated as

\displaystyle 1-|\hat{e}\cdot\hat{q}^{\prime}|^{2}={}

\displaystyle 1-|e_{x}|^{2}\sin^{2}\theta^{\prime}\cos^{2}\phi^{\prime}-|e_{y}|^{2}\sin^{2}\theta^{\prime}\sin^{2}\phi^{\prime}-|e_{z}|^{2}\cos^{2}\theta^{\prime}

(314)

\displaystyle-2\,{\text{Re}\;}\!\left(e_{x}\overline{e}_{y}\right)\sin^{2}\theta^{\prime}\cos\phi^{\prime}\sin\phi^{\prime}

\displaystyle-2\,{\text{Re}\;}\!\left(e_{x}\overline{e}_{z}\right)\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\phi^{\prime}

\displaystyle-2\,{\text{Re}\;}\!\left(e_{y}\overline{e}_{z}\right)\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime}\sin\phi^{\prime}.

Note that besides
1-\left|\mathbf{e}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{q}}^{\prime}\right|^{2}
,
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
only appears in
(\cos\phi^{\prime}\sin\phi-\sin\phi^{\prime}\cos\phi)
and in the velocity. If we look at
j_{z}
, then
\int d\phi d\phi^{\prime}(1-\left|\mathbf{e}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{q}}^{\prime}\right|^{2})(\cos\phi^{\prime}\sin\phi-\sin\phi^{\prime}\cos\phi)=0
. If we look at
j_{x}
, only the term
-2\,{\text{Re}\;}\!\left(e_{y}\overline{e}_{z}\right)\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime}\sin\phi^{\prime}
in
(1-\left|\mathbf{e}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{q}}^{\prime}\right|^{2})
and
-2{\text{Im}\;}(e_{z}\overline{e}_{x})\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\phi^{\prime}
in
i(\hat{e}\times\hat{\overline{e}})\cdot\hat{q}^{\prime}
will contribute, and thus

\displaystyle j_{x}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\frac{|e|^{3}}{\hbar^{2}}\tau_{\text{eff}}^{2}\left(\frac{e^{2}}{\epsilon}\right)^{3}n_{\text{imp}}\frac{1}{k_{scr}^{6}}|\mathbf{E}|^{2}\times\frac{1}{32}\left(\frac{\omega}{2v_{F}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{\hbar(\hbar v)^{2}}2\pi^{3}

(315)

\displaystyle\times\bigg\{2\,{\text{Re}\;}\!\left(e_{y}e_{z}^{*}\right)\int d\theta d\theta^{\prime}d\theta^{\prime\prime}\frac{\sin^{3}\theta}{(1+\alpha\cos\theta)^{3}}\sin^{3}\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime}(1+\alpha\cos\theta^{\prime})^{4}\frac{\sin\theta^{\prime\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime\prime}}{(1+\alpha\cos\theta^{\prime\prime})^{3}}

\displaystyle+2\,{\text{Im}\;}\!\left(e_{z}e_{x}^{*}\right)\int d\theta d\theta^{\prime}d\theta^{\prime\prime}\frac{\sin^{3}\theta}{(1+\alpha\cos\theta)^{3}}\sin^{3}\theta^{\prime}(1+\alpha\cos\theta^{\prime})^{4}\frac{\sin\theta^{\prime\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime\prime}}{(1+\alpha\cos\theta^{\prime\prime})^{3}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{|e|^{3}}{\hbar^{2}}\tau_{\text{eff}}^{2}\left(\frac{e^{2}/\epsilon}{\hbar v}\right)^{3}\frac{n_{\text{imp}}}{k_{scr}^{6}}\left(\frac{\omega}{2v_{F}}\right)^{2}v\big(f_{1}(\alpha)\,{\text{Re}\;}\!\left(e_{y}\overline{e}_{z}\right)-f_{2}(\alpha)\,{\text{Im}\;}\!\left(e_{z}\overline{e}_{x}\right)\big),

where
f^{l}(\alpha)=\frac{1}{105}\frac{(\text{arctanh}(\alpha)-\alpha/(1-\alpha^{2}))(7+3\alpha^{2})}{\alpha(1-\alpha^{2})^{2}}
and
f^{c}(\alpha)=\frac{35+3a^{2}(14+a^{2})}{105}\frac{(\alpha/(1-\alpha^{2})-\text{arctanh}(\alpha))}{4\alpha^{2}(1-\alpha^{2})^{2}}
.

Similarly, if we look at
j_{y}
, only
-2\,{\text{Re}\;}\!\left(e_{x}\overline{e}_{z}\right)\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\phi^{\prime}
in
1-|\hat{e}\cdot\hat{q}^{\prime}|^{2}
and
2{\text{Im}\;}(e_{z}\overline{e}_{y})\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\phi^{\prime}
in
i(\hat{e}\times\hat{\overline{e}})\cdot\hat{q}^{\prime}
will contribute and we get

j_{y}=-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{|e|^{3}}{\hbar^{2}}\tau_{\text{eff}}^{2}\left(\frac{e^{2}/\epsilon}{\hbar v}\right)^{3}\frac{n_{\text{imp}}}{k_{scr}^{6}}\left(\frac{\omega}{2v_{F}}\right)^{2}v\big(f_{1}(\alpha)\,{\text{Re}\;}\!\left(e_{x}\overline{e}_{z}\right)+f_{2}(\alpha)\,{\text{Im}\;}\!\left(e_{z}\overline{e}_{y}\right)\big),

(316)

which matches the symmetry analysis.

Evaluation of the second term is more tricky, but fortunately it is always zero. To see this, we need to first calculate
[|\hat{e}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{cv,\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}|^{2}\delta(2\hbar v_{F}q^{\prime}-\hbar\omega)]

\displaystyle=\frac{\int d\mathbf{k}|\hat{e}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{cv,\mathbf{k}}|^{2}\delta(2\hbar v_{F}k-\hbar\omega)\delta(E_{c\mathbf{k}}-E_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime}})}{\int d\mathbf{k}\delta(E_{c\mathbf{k}}-E_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime}})}

(317)

The numerator can be calculated through

\displaystyle\int d\mathbf{k}|\hat{e}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{cv,\mathbf{k}}|^{2}\delta(2\hbar v_{F}k-\hbar\omega)\delta(E_{c\mathbf{k}}-E_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime}})

(318)

\displaystyle=\int\sin\theta d\theta dk\frac{2\pi}{4}\left(1-|e_{z}|^{2}\cos^{2}\theta-\frac{|e_{x}|^{2}+|e_{y}|^{2}}{2}\sin^{2}\theta-2{\text{Im}\;}(e_{x}\overline{e}_{y})\cos\theta\right)\delta(2\hbar v_{F}k-\hbar\omega)\delta(E_{c\mathbf{k}}-E_{c\mathbf{q}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\pi}{4}\frac{1}{\hbar v_{F}}\frac{1}{2\hbar v_{F}}\int d\cos\theta\left(1-|e_{z}|^{2}\cos^{2}\theta-\frac{|e_{x}|^{2}+|e_{y}|^{2}}{2}\sin^{2}\theta-2{\text{Im}\;}(e_{x}\overline{e}_{y})\right)\delta(\frac{\omega}{2v_{F}}(1+\alpha\cos\theta)-q^{\prime}({1+\alpha\cos\theta^{\prime})})

which is
\phi^{\prime}
independent. Thus, the integral over
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
will always lead to zero due to the presence of
(\sin\phi\cos\phi^{\prime}-\sin\phi^{\prime}\cos\phi)
.

Anisotropic Weyl with tilting.–

Now, let us consider a more general case where

H=\hbar(f_{1}(k_{x}\sigma_{x}+k_{y}\sigma_{y})+vk_{z}\sigma_{z})

(319)

with
f_{1}\neq v
. For simplicity we can define

(\tilde{k}_{x},\tilde{k}_{y},\tilde{k}_{z})=(f_{1}k_{x},f_{1}k_{y},vk_{z})

(320)

With this new coordinate, we go back to the isotropic case, where tilting along any direction should be equivalent. After this rescale, the only thing changed in the current is a factor.

n-Weyl fermions with tilting.–

The Hamiltonian of a n-Weyl fermion is given by

H=\hbar\mathbf{d}\cdot\boldsymbol{\sigma},\ \mathbf{d}=(f_{n}{\text{Re}\;}(k_{+}^{n}),f_{n}{\text{Im}\;}(k_{+}^{n}),vk_{z}).

(321)

Following the procedure discussed above, let us first consider the tilting along
z
-direction. We look at the term proportional to affinity because the term proportional to the average of the affinity is again
\phi^{\prime}
independent and will vanish. Similarly, the imaginary part of the Bargamann invariant only contributes

-\frac{1}{4}\hat{d}_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime},z}(\hat{d}_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}\times\hat{d}_{\mathbf{q}})_{z}=-\frac{1}{4}\frac{vq_{z}^{\prime\prime}}{d_{\mathbf{q}}d_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}d_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}}}\rho^{n}\rho^{\prime n}(\cos n\phi^{\prime}\sin n\phi-\sin n\phi^{\prime}\cos n\phi).

However, remember that in all other terms of the integrand, only the velocity term contributes harmonics of
\phi
and only the optical affinity term contributes harmonics of
\phi^{\prime}
. Since the velocity term only contributes
\cos\phi
and
\sin\phi
, the integral over
\phi
will always vanish because the
\phi
from Bargamann invariant arises as higher harmonics when
n>1
. Thus, for tilting along z, the current vanishes.

Next, let us consider tilting along x, the only different thing happens in the Dirac-delta function – It now contributes the
\cos\phi
and
\cos\phi^{\prime}
. The integral can then be expressed as

\displaystyle\int d\phi d\phi^{\prime}d\phi^{\prime\prime}(\cos n\phi^{\prime}\sin n\phi-\sin n\phi^{\prime}\cos n\phi)\int\rho d\rho dq_{z}\rho^{\prime}d\rho^{\prime}dq_{z}^{\prime}\rho^{\prime\prime}d\rho^{\prime\prime}dq_{z}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{v}_{c}\left(-\frac{1}{4}\frac{vq_{z}^{\prime\prime}}{d_{\mathbf{q}}d_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}d_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}}}\rho^{n}\rho^{\prime n}\right)|\hat{e}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{cv,\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}|^{2}

(322)

\displaystyle\times\delta(2\hbar d_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}-\hbar\omega)\delta(\hbar(d_{\mathbf{q}}+v_{xt}\rho\cos\phi)-\hbar(d_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime\prime}}+v_{xt}\rho^{\prime\prime}\cos\phi^{\prime\prime}))\delta(\hbar(d_{\mathbf{q}}+v_{xt}\rho\cos\phi)-\hbar(d_{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}+v_{xt}\rho^{\prime}\cos\phi^{\prime}))

After integrating out the Dirac- delta function, all
\cos\phi
and
\cos\phi^{\prime\prime}
arise from the process can be replaced by a function of
\cos\phi^{\prime},\rho,\rho^{\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime},k_{z},k_{z}^{\prime},k_{z}^{\prime\prime}
. As a result, the integral over
\phi
will always be zero. Thus, no matter whether for x-tilted or z-tilted n-Weyl fermions, the ballistic photocurrent is zero.

C.6
Appendix to ‘Warp-activated
\boldsymbol{j}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\text{ballistic}}}
for massive
n
-Dirac fermions’

In this appendix, we consider the details of the calculations for the warped
n
-Dirac Hamiltonian relevant for
n>1
-layer rhombohedral graphene
41
,

\displaystyle H_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{d}\cdot\bm{\sigma}=\begin{pmatrix}\Delta&f_{n}(k_{-})^{n}\\
f_{n}(k_{+})^{n}&-\Delta\end{pmatrix}+H^{6^{\prime},n}_{\boldsymbol{k}},

(323)

\displaystyle k_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k_{x}\pm ik_{y},\quad\Delta=m_{D}v^{2},

(324)

see also Eq. (
106
).
The trigonal warping here originates from the skewed interlayer coupling and the next-nearest interlayer coupling.

In what follows, we focus on the effects of warping and assume the simplest monopolar impurity potential. In addition, we work in the low-frequency regime where the warping effect on the energy-momentum dispersion is small. Thus we retain only the terms linear in the warping strength. In this case, we expand
E_{\boldsymbol{k}}\approx E_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{(0)}+E_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{(1)}
where
E_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{(1)}
quantifies the effects of the warping term.

The imaginary part of the Bargmann invariant for a generic two-band system is

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}{\mathscr{B}}_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}=-\frac{\left[\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}\times\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{\boldsymbol{k}}\right]\cdot\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}}{4}.

(325)

For monopolar impurities, the integral over
\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}
reduces to

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}k^{\prime\prime}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\mbox{Im}{{\mathscr{B}}_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}}\,\delta{\left(E_{\rm ex}-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}\right)}=-\frac{\left[\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}\times\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{\boldsymbol{k}}\right]_{z}}{4}\int\frac{d^{2}k^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{d_{3,\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}}{d_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}}\,\delta{\left(E_{\rm ex}-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(0)}-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(1)}\right)}

\displaystyle\approx-\frac{\left[\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}\times\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{\boldsymbol{k}}\right]_{z}}{4}\int\frac{d^{2}k^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{d_{3,\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}}{E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(0)}}\left[1+\frac{E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(1)}}{E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(0)}}\right]\left[\,\delta{\left(E_{\rm ex}-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(0)}\right)}-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(1)}\frac{d}{dE_{\rm ex}}\,\delta{\left(E_{\rm ex}-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(0)}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\approx-\frac{\left[\mathbf{d}_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}\times\mathbf{d}_{\boldsymbol{k}}\right]_{z}}{4E_{\rm ex}^{2}}\frac{\Delta}{E_{\rm ex}}\nu(E_{\rm ex})\Bigg\{1+\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{d\varphi^{\prime\prime}}{2\pi}\frac{E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(1)}}{E_{\rm ex}}\Big|_{k^{\prime\prime}\to k_{\rm ex}}-\frac{1}{2\pi\nu(E_{\rm ex})}\frac{1}{|dE_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(0)}/dk^{\prime\prime}|}\left(\frac{d}{dk^{\prime\prime}}k^{\prime\prime}\frac{1}{dE_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(0)}/dk^{\prime\prime}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{d\varphi^{\prime\prime}}{2\pi}E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\Big|_{k^{\prime\prime}\to k_{\rm ex}}\Bigg\}

with
\nu(E_{\rm ex})=E_{\rm ex}/(2n\pi f_{n}^{2}k_{\rm ex}^{(2n-2)})
being the DOS and the excitation vector
k_{\rm ex}=\left[(E_{\rm ex}^{2}-\Delta^{2})/f_{n}^{2}\right]^{1/(2n)}
calculated without warping.
For the
n
-Dirac Hamiltonian, we have
dE_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}^{(0)}/dk^{\prime\prime}\Big|_{k^{\prime\prime}\to k_{\rm ex}}=\frac{nf_{n}^{2}k_{\rm ex}^{2n-1}}{E_{\rm ex}}
.

In calculating the warping effects in other terms, we use

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}k^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{2}}F_{\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}\,\delta{\left(E_{\boldsymbol{k}}-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}\right)}\approx\int\frac{d^{2}k^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{2}}F_{\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}\left[\,\delta{\left(E_{\boldsymbol{k}}-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{(0)}\right)}+\left(-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\frac{d}{dE_{\boldsymbol{k}}}\,\delta{\left(E_{\boldsymbol{k}}-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{(0)}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=\int\frac{d^{2}k^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{2}}F_{\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}\,\delta{\left(E_{\boldsymbol{k}}-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{(0)}\right)}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}dk^{\prime}\left[\frac{d}{dk^{\prime}}\frac{dk^{\prime}}{dE_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{(0)}}k^{\prime}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{d\varphi^{\prime\prime}}{2\pi}F_{\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}\left(-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\,\delta{\left(E_{\boldsymbol{k}}-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{(0)}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=\int\frac{d^{2}k^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{2}}F_{\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}\,\delta{\left(E_{\boldsymbol{k}}-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{(0)}\right)}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\left[\frac{d}{dk^{\prime}}\frac{dk^{\prime}}{dE_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{(0)}}k^{\prime}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{d\varphi^{\prime\prime}}{2\pi}F_{\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}\left(-E_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\right]\frac{1}{|dE_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{(0)}/dk^{\prime}|}\Big|_{k^{\prime}\to k_{\rm ex}}.

(327)

We use the above expressions in Eq. (
38
), which, assuming monopolar disorded
\widetilde{V}_{\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}=V_{0}
reads as

\displaystyle\mathbf{j}_{\text{ballistic}}

\displaystyle=-2_{\text{e-h}}\frac{4\pi^{3}|e|^{3}}{\hbar^{2}}(\tau^{mr}_{E_{ex}})^{2}n_{\text{imp}}|\mathbf{E}|^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}}\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{k}}\;\operatorname{Im}{\mathscr{B}}_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}}\delta(E_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}})\,\delta(E_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}})\;\delta|\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{cv,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}|^{2}\,\delta(E_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}-E_{ex}).

(328)

A particularly simple expression for the ballistic current response tensor is obtained for the following warping term
H^{6^{\prime},n}_{\boldsymbol{k}}=h_{1,n-2,0}k_{-}^{n-2}k_{+}\sigma_{+}+h.c.
,

\displaystyle B^{\text{de}}_{abc}=2_{\rm p-h}|e|^{3}(\tau^{mr}_{E_{\rm ex}})^{2}\frac{n_{\rm imp}V_{0}^{3}}{2\pi}\left[\frac{E_{\rm ex}^{2}-\Delta^{2}}{f_{n}^{2}}\right]^{-2+2/n}\frac{E_{\rm ex}^{2}}{n^{2}f_{n}^{2}}\frac{\pi h_{1,n-2,0}\Delta}{16f_{n}}\widetilde{B}_{abc}^{(n)},

(329)

where the nontrivial coefficients

are listed in Tab.

.

) for the warped

One can understand the vanishing current at
n\neq 1
and
n\neq 2
as the result of the mismatch between angular harmonics in the velocity, Bargmann invariant, and affinity deviant. Indeed, we have the following harmonics

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{\boldsymbol{k}}

\displaystyle\propto

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}=0,\pm 1,\ldots}\left(\epsilon_{l_{1}}^{\prime}(k)\,\hat{\mathbf{k}}+\frac{3l_{1}}{k}\epsilon_{l_{1}}(k)\,\hat{\mathbf{\varphi}}\right)e^{3il_{1}\varphi}=\sum_{l_{1}=0,\pm 1,\ldots}\sum_{\pm}\left(A_{l,\pm}\hat{\mathbf{x}}+B_{l,\pm}\hat{\mathbf{y}}\right)e^{i(\pm 1+3l_{1})\varphi},

(330)

\displaystyle\delta|\mathcal{A}_{cv,\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}|^{2}

(332)

where the first expression in the first equation contains the radial and azimuthal components of the velocity

To derive the angular expansion of the velocity, we used the symmetry property of the energy
\epsilon(k,\varphi)=\epsilon(k,\varphi+2\pi/3)
and expanded
\epsilon(k,\varphi)=\sum_{l_{1}=0,\pm 1,\ldots}\epsilon_{l_{1}}(k)e^{3il_{1}\varphi}
.
. After integrating over

and
\varphi
